L1361: ## 9.2 Labeling Recommendations
L1362: The Warnings and Precautions statements should be re-ordered as follows:
L1363: 5.1 (b) (4)
5.2 Concomitant Use with Central Nervous System Depressants
5.3 Withdrawal
5.4 Physical and Psychological Dependence
5.5 Suicidal Behavior and Ideation
L1364: The Warnings and Precautions statement about withdrawal (5.3) should be reworded to  read as follows:
L1365: ## ** 5.3 Withdrawal **
L1366: (b) (4)
L1367: As with all antiepileptic drugs, ONFI should be withdrawn gradually to minimize  the risk of precipitating seizures, seizure exacerbation, or status epilepticus.
L1368: Withdrawal symptoms, (e.g., convulsions, psychosis, hallucinations, behavioral  disorder, tremor, and anxiety) have been reported following abrupt  discontinuance of benzodiazepines. The more severe withdrawal symptoms have  usually been limited to those patients who received excessive doses over an  extended period of time followed by an abrupt discontinuation. Generally milder  withdrawal symptoms (e.g., dysphoria, anxiety, and insomnia) have been  reported following abrupt discontinuance of benzodiazepines taken continuously  at therapeutic doses for several months.
L1369: (b) (4)
L1370: (b) (4)
L1371: In section 7, Drug Interactions, Lundbeck should include the following language:
L1372: Strong inhibitors of CYP2C19 may result in increased exposure to N-CLB, the active metabolite of clobazam. Dosage adjustment of ONFI may be necessary when coadministered with strong CYP2C19 inhibitors (e.g. fluconazole, fluvoxamine, ticlopidine) (b) (4).
L1373: ## 9.3 Advisory Committee Meeting
L1374: The Division did not present the clobazam NDA to an Advisory Committee.

L1379: Figure 1 is a schematic diagram illustrating the study design for Study OV-1012, divided into three phases: Baseline, Titration, and Maintenance*. The timeline runs from Day -28 to Week 15, with key points marked at Day 0, Week 3, and Week 15.
The Baseline phase spans 4 weeks (Day -28 to Day 0). The Titration phase spans 3 weeks (Day 0 to Week 3). The Maintenance* phase spans 12 weeks (Week 3 to Week 15).
Subjects were stratified into two weight groups and randomly assigned to one of four treatment arms during the Maintenance phase:
High Dose
Medium Dose
Low Dose
Placebo
At the end of the Maintenance phase (Week 15), subjects could proceed to either:
Open-Label Extension Study (0.5 mg/kg/day start)
OR
Taper Period (2 or 3 weeks)
Subjects not directly continuing into the open-label extension study tapered off study drug in a 2- or 3-week taper period, with the final visit 1 week post last dose.
L1380: Figure 4 is a schematic diagram illustrating the study design for Study OV-1002, divided into four phases: Baseline, Titration, Maintenance*, and Open-label. The timeline spans from Week -4 to Week 10.
Baseline: 4 weeks (Weeks -4 to Day 0).
Titration: 3 weeks (Day 0 to Week 3).
Maintenance*: 4 weeks (Week 3 to Week 7). This phase is split into two dose arms: High Dose and Low Dose.
Open-label: Starts at Week 7. This phase includes an Extension period and a Taper period, ending at Week 10.
The diagram shows that subjects in the High Dose arm and Low Dose arm both transition into the Open-label Extension phase. Subjects in the Low Dose arm also transition into the Taper phase, which ends at Week 10.

L1382: Figure 3
Time to Onset of the First Treatment-emergent Pneumonia-related Adverse Event During All Clobazam Treatment in Phase 2/3 LGS Studies
Safety Population
Kaplan-Meier survival curve showing the time to onset of the first treatment-emergent pneumonia-related adverse event (AE) during all clobazam treatment in the Phase 2/3 LGS Studies Safety Population. The X-axis represents Time to Onset of the First Treatment-emergent Pneumonia-related AE (Days), ranging from 0 to 2000. The Y-axis represents Survival Probability, ranging from 0.0 to 1.0. The curve starts at 1.0 and decreases rapidly, reaching a plateau around 0.75 survival probability after approximately 1000 days. The legend indicates '+ Censored' data points.
L1383: Kaplan-Meier survival curve showing the time to onset of the first treatment-emergent somnolence-related adverse event (AE) in double-blind Phase 2/3 LGS studies. The X-axis is Time to Onset (Days) from 0 to 150. The Y-axis is Survival Probability from 0.0 to 1.0. Four groups are compared: 0.25 mg/kg CLOBAZAM (solid line), 0.50 mg/kg CLOBAZAM (dashed line), 1.00 mg/kg CLOBAZAM (dotted line), and PLACEBO (dash-dot line). The survival probability decreases rapidly for all groups, with the placebo group showing the highest survival probability (lowest event rate) after 25 days. The legend indicates '+ Censored' data points.
